Reliability: An official government statement, good for what Spain said, but it is self-presentation and does not show what Spain did at sea.
- **Estado Mayor de la Defensa (2025). Operation Atalanta news pages, 25 April and 2 October 2025.** EMAD (Spanish Defence Staff). [link](https://emad.defensa.gob.es/en/operaciones/operaciones-en-el-exterior/42-ATALANTA/Noticias/listado/250425-atalanta-visita-delegacion-europea-somali-fragata.html) — **Grade B1**. Summary: Atalanta is run from Rota until at least February 2027; Spain gives about 350 personnel, a frigate, special forces and a patrol aircraft in Djibouti. Reliability: An official Spanish source, authoritative on Spain's contribution, but its claim to be the only uninterrupted EU contributor is self-reported.

### Historical news, 1967–75

- **Associated Press (1975). [Sadat reopens the Suez Canal].** *The Michigan Daily*, 6 June. [link](https://digital.bentley.umich.edu/midaily/mdp.39015071754639/265) — **Grade B1**. Summary: Reports Sadat leading a convoy, with the US cruiser *Little Rock*, to reopen the canal on 5 June 1975, eight years to the day after it closed. Reliability: AP wire copy written at the time and reprinted in a student paper's free archive; the exact headline is unconfirmed and it records the event, not why it happened.
- **Associated Press (2021). Not only grounded ships: A look at past crises that closed Suez.** *Times of Israel*, March. [link](https://www.timesofisrael.com/not-only-grounded-ships-a-look-at-past-crises-that-closed-suez/) — **Grade B1**. Summary: Recalls the eight-year closure after 1967 and the stranded "Yellow Fleet"; a UN study put the cost at $1.7 billion a year in lost trade and $250 million a year in lost Egyptian tolls. Reliability: AP copy written as a 2021 retrospective, not at the time; trace the UN study before citing.
- **Time (c. 1969). World: The Suez Canal's Bleak Centennial.** *Time*. [link](https://time.com/archive/6635732/world-the-suez-canals-bleak-centennial/) — **Grade B2**. Summary: At the canal's 100th anniversary, the only ships left were 15 vessels trapped since 1967, 14 in the Great Bitter Lake, with skeleton crews rotated every three months. Reliability: A leading US news weekly writing at the time, but the issue date is inferred and ship counts vary between sources.
- **Time (1967). Middle East: The Quickest War.** *Time*, June. [link](https://time.com/archive/6890621/middle-east-the-quickest-war/) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Coverage of the Six-Day War that reports the canal "blocked at both ends by scuttled Egyptian ships", the start of the closure. Reliability: Contemporary reporting, but it is unclear whether this piece or the linked essay "Running From Defeat" holds the line, and dates are unconfirmed.
- **Time (1974). Egypt: Clear the Canal / Egypt: Salvaging Suez (issue of 6 May 1974).** *Time*. [link](https://time.com/archive/6611227/egypt-clear-the-canal/) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Reports the start of the US-led clearance with British minesweepers, hunting "thousands of shells, rockets and bombs"; the US share of the cost was about $15 million. Reliability: Contemporary weekly reporting, but it is unclear which page holds which figure and the $15 million is not confirmed elsewhere.
- **Time (1975). Middle East: A Watershed Week for Egypt's Sadat / Egypt: The Canal Reborn.** *Time*, June. [link](https://time.com/archive/6846857/middle-east-a-watershed-week-for-egypts-sadat/) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Sadat timed the reopening to the anniversary of the closure, after US-brokered talks; Israeli-flagged ships stayed barred and about 275,000 people had returned to Port Said. Reliability: Contemporary but US-centred reporting; the issue dates are not confirmed.
- **Reuters (1975). Lebanon: Prime Minister designate Rachid Karami praises Egypt on reopening of Suez Canal [newsreel].** Reuters Screenocean archive. [link](https://reuters.screenocean.com/record/231153) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Agency film of Arab reaction to the reopening; a related record notes the canal "has been closed since the 1967 Middle East war". Reliability: A major news agency's own archive, but only catalogue text is visible without a login, so it adds little.
- **Al Jazeera World (2019). Suez: The Yellow Fleet trapped by the 1967 Arab-Israeli War [documentary].** *Al Jazeera*, 20 November. [link](https://www.aljazeera.com/amp/video/al-jazeera-world/2019/11/20/suez-the-yellow-fleet-trapped-by-the-1967-arab-israeli-war) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Tells the story of the 15 merchant ships from eight countries trapped from 1967 to 1975, whose crews held a "Bitter Lake Olympics"; most had to be towed out. Reliability: A Qatari state-funded broadcaster's retrospective made for a general audience, not a primary account.
- **UPI (1975). Foreign Affairs: Peace in the Middle East, Conflict in Ireland, Death of Franco [audio review].** *United Press International*. [link](https://www.upi.com/Archives/Audio/1975/Foreign-Affaris-Peace-in-the-Middle-East-Conflict-in-Ireland-Death-of-Franco/) — **Grade B2**. Summary: UPI's review of 1975 notes no Israeli ships were allowed through the reopened canal, though other ships carrying supplies to Israel passed. Reliability: A major wire service at the time, but it is audio and only the page description was seen.
- **Time (1973). Middle East: The War of the Day of Judgment.** *Time*, October. [link](https://time.com/archive/6841881/middle-east-the-war-of-the-day-of-judgment/) — **Grade B3**. Summary: Covers the 1973 war, when up to 100,000 Egyptian troops crossed the canal and Arab oil producers cut output, with US oil prices roughly quadrupling. Reliability: Contemporary weekly coverage, but the dates are unreliable and the "quadrupled" line may come from a 2013 retrospective.
- **Time (1975). The World: Suez: The Seas Rejoined.** *Time*, 9 June. [link](https://time.com/archive/6846859/the-world-suez-the-seas-rejoined/) — **Grade B3**. Summary: After more than a year of multinational clearance, the canal reopened; a UN estimate put the closure's cost to the world economy at about $10 billion. Reliability: Contemporary, but the $10 billion is a second-hand UN estimate and some figures may come from neighbouring Time pages.
- **Time (1974). The World: Clean Sweep of the Canal.** *Time*, December. [link](https://time.com/archive/6876758/the-world-clean-sweep-of-the-canal/) — **Grade B4**. Summary: Reports the end of the clearance, with more than 700,000 explosives disarmed and 16 ships "rusting but still afloat" in the Great Bitter Lake. Reliability: Contemporary, but its figures conflict with another Time piece (686,000) and with naval records, so use USNI Proceedings instead.
- **Arndt, R. (1975). Suez: The Reopening.** *Aramco World* 26(5), September/October. [link](https://archive.aramcoworld.com/issue/197505/suez-the.reopening.htm) — **Grade C1**. Summary: An eyewitness account of a seven-ship flotilla sailing from Port Said to Ismailia in five hours on 5 June 1975, "exactly eight years" after the closure. Reliability: A Saudi Aramco company magazine with an interest in the canal and a celebratory tone, though its key facts are confirmed elsewhere.
- **Samuelson, M. (1975). Suez Canal.** *The Spectator*, 7 June, p. 11. [link](https://archive.spectator.co.uk/article/7th-june-1975/11/suez-canal) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Calls the reopening "one of the more significant anti-climaxes of our time", because shipping had adapted to the Cape route with supertankers. Reliability: A right-leaning British weekly's opinion piece written at the time; a useful contemporary judgement but not a source of data.
- **Quartz (2021). The Suez Canal's worst stuck ship fiasco lasted eight years.** *Quartz*, March. [link](https://qz.com/1988437/the-suez-canals-worst-stuck-ship-fiasco-lasted-eight-years) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Uses the 2021 *Ever Given* blockage as a hook to retell the Yellow Fleet story, linking the two Suez periods; no new figure. Reliability: A US business site's secondary retelling written to attract readers; check its figures against older sources.
- **Ara (2021). Nos importa Suez [opinion].** *Ara* (Spanish edition). [link](https://es.ara.cat/opinion/importa-suez_129_3933483.amp.html) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Recalls that during the 1967–75 closure supertankers were built to make the long route round Africa pay, an example of technology adapting to disruption. Reliability: An opinion column in a Catalan centre-left paper; follow the idea up in academic history before citing.
- **Al-Ahram Weekly (n.d.). The Suez Canal: A waterway to die for.** *Ahram Online*. [link](https://english.ahram.org.eg/NewsContentP/50/408198/AlAhram-Weekly/The-Suez-Canal-A-waterway-to-die-for.aspx) — **Grade D1**. Summary: A history feature noting the canal stayed shut for eight years after 1967 until Sadat reopened it on 5 June 1975, the same date it closed. Reliability: Egyptian state-owned media with a nationalist framing; the dates are well confirmed, but treat its tone as official narrative.
- **Egypt Today (2021). In its 152nd anniversary: landmarks in history of Egypt's Suez Canal.** *Egypt Today*, November. [link](https://www.egypttoday.com/Article/1/110011/In-its-152nd-anniversary-landmarks-in-history-of-Egypt-s) — **Grade D2**. Summary: A timeline that presents the 1975 reopening as a peace gesture following talks with Israel; no figure. Reliability: A state-aligned Egyptian outlet giving the official story, so use it for Egypt's framing, not as fact.

### News and trade press, 2023–26

- **CNN (2023). Austin announces US-led security operation focusing on Red Sea, Gulf of Aden after Houthi attacks on commercial shipping.** *KTVZ / CNN*, 18 December. [link](https://ktvz.com/politics/cnn-us-politics/2023/12/18/austin-announces-us-led-security-operation-focusing-on-red-sea-gulf-of-aden-after-houthi-attacks-on-commercial-shipping/) — **Grade B1**. Summary: The US launched Operation Prosperity Guardian under Task Force 153, about a month after the *Galaxy Leader* hijack, naming ten countries including the US. Reliability: Major US network reporting a Pentagon announcement; its partner list includes Spain, which then declined to join.
- **Reuters (2023). Attacks in Red Sea force Maersk to reroute vessels around Africa.** *BusinessLIVE*, 19 December. [link](https://www.businesslive.co.za/bd/world/2023-12-19-attacks-in-red-sea-force-maersk-to-reroute-vessels-around-africa/) — **Grade B1**. Summary: Maersk, the world's number-two container line, sent Red Sea-bound ships round the Cape; about 20 of its ships had paused after a near-miss on 15 December. Reliability: Reuters wire copy reprinted in a South African paper; Maersk's quotes are corporate messaging.
- **Boston Globe (2024). Maersk decides the Red Sea is too unsafe for its ships for now.** *The Boston Globe* (Bloomberg/AP), 2 January. [link](https://www.bostonglobe.com/2024/01/02/business/maersk-decides-red-sea-is-too-unsafe-its-ships-now) — **Grade B1**. Summary: Maersk halted Red Sea transits again after four Houthi small boats tried to board the *Maersk Hangzhou* on 31 December 2023, reversing its planned return. Reliability: Bloomberg and AP copy in a major US paper, possibly paywalled; seen as a snippet.
- **Associated Press (2024). Ship earlier hit by Yemen's Houthi rebels sinks in Red Sea, first vessel lost in conflict.** *The Boston Globe*, 2 March. [link](https://www.bostonglobe.com/2024/03/02/world/ship-earlier-hit-by-yemens-houthi-rebels-sinks-red-sea-first-vessel-lost-conflict) — **Grade B1**. Summary: The *Rubymar*, hit by a missile on 18 February 2024, sank with about 21,000 tonnes of fertiliser on board. Reliability: AP wire copy confirmed elsewhere; the environmental warning came from US Central Command, a party to the conflict.
- **Al Jazeera (2024). Greek-owned Tutor believed to have sunk a week after Houthi missile attack.** *Al Jazeera*, 19 June. [link](https://www.aljazeera.com/news/2024/6/19/greek-owned-tutor-believed-to-have-sunk-a-week-after-houthi-missile-attack) — **Grade B1**. Summary: The coal carrier *Tutor* was the first ship hit by a Houthi drone boat; one seafarer died and 21 were rescued by US and French forces. Reliability: A Qatari state-funded broadcaster, but the sinking was confirmed by UKMTO and the salvage firm.
- **Reuters (2025). Houthi terrorists free crew of Galaxy Leader after more than a year in captivity.** *Baird Maritime* / *Gulf News*, January. [link](https://www.bairdmaritime.com/amp/story/security/incidents/piracy/houthi-terrorists-free-crew-of-galaxy-leader-after-more-than-a-year-in-captivity) — **Grade B1**. Summary: The 25 crew were handed to Oman after about 14 months, a release the Houthis linked to the Gaza ceasefire of 19 January 2025. Reliability: Reuters copy reprinted by trade press; "terrorists" is Baird's loaded wording, not Reuters'.
- **CBS News (2025). Trump says U.S. will stop bombing Houthis after group "capitulated".** *CBS News*, 6 May. [link](https://www.cbsnews.com/amp/news/trump-houthis-ceasefire-bombing-over-capitulated) — **Grade B1**. Summary: Trump ended the US bombing campaign after Oman brokered a deal in which neither side would target the other in the Red Sea and Bab el-Mandeb. Reliability: A major US network relaying the president's own framing, so read it with the Houthi statement below.
- **Reuters (2025). Houthis say ceasefire agreement with US does not apply to Israel-linked targets, says spokesperson.** *Malay Mail*, 7 May. [link](https://malaymail.com/news/world/2025/05/07/houthis-say-ceasefire-agreement-with-us-does-not-apply-to-israel-linked-targets-says-spokesperson/175925) — **Grade B1**. Summary: The chief Houthi negotiator said the truce "does not include Israel in any way", so it protected US ships but not all shipping. Reliability: Reuters copy of a party's own statement, good evidence of their position but not of their behaviour.
- **Bloomberg (2025). Houthi attacks in Red Sea sink two cargo ships, leave three dead.** *Bloomberg*, 7 July. [link](https://www.bloomberg.com/news/articles/2025-07-07/houthis-say-they-hit-red-sea-ship-in-first-attack-this-year) — **Grade B1**. Summary: The first Houthi attacks of 2025, two months after the US truce, sank the *Magic Seas* and the *Eternity C*, with three dead at the time of writing. Reliability: A major financial news agency, paywalled; the headline was probably updated and the death toll later rose.
- **Associated Press (2026). Yemen's Iranian-backed Houthis say they will block Saudi shipping at Red Sea gateway.** *ABC News*, 20 July. [link](https://abcnews.com/International/wireStory/yemens-houthis-announce-maritime-embargo-saudi-arabia-134912678) — **Grade B1**. Summary: The Houthis announced a "maritime embargo" on Saudi Arabia, saying Bab el-Mandeb would be "closed to the Saudis" just as Riyadh relied on Red Sea ports to bypass Hormuz. Reliability: AP wire copy confirmed by other searches; the Houthi statements are claims and no traffic data were given.
- **Reuters (2026). Red Sea war insurance costs rise after Houthi blockade, sources say.** *Insurance Journal*, 21 July. [link](https://www.insurancejournal.com/news/international/2026/07/21/878345.htm) — **Grade B1**. Summary: A day after the blockade was declared, indicative Red Sea war-risk premiums more than doubled, from about 0.3% to about 0.75% of hull value. Reliability: Reuters copy agreeing with other Reuters reports, but the figures are broker quotes.
- **Reuters (2026). Houthis' Red Sea attacks prompt London insurers to widen high-risk zone.** *Insurance Journal*, 30 July. [link](https://www.insurancejournal.com/news/international/2026/07/30/879653.htm) — **Grade B1**. Summary: London insurers widened the Red Sea listed area to Saudi ports, and premiums for Jeddah and Yanbu quadrupled from 0.25% to 1% of hull value within a day. Reliability: Reuters copy matching the JWC circular, though premiums are broker quotes, not prices paid.
- **Lloyd's List (2026). Tanker hit as Houthis launch attacks on Saudi-linked vessels.** *Lloyd's List*, c. 23 July. [link](https://www.lloydslist.com/LL1157955/Tanker-hit-as-Houthis-launch-attacks-on-Saudi-linked-vessels) — **Grade B1**. Summary: The Saudi-flagged tanker *Encelia* was hit by projectiles in the first attack of the new Houthi blockade. Reliability: The leading shipping paper of record, paywalled; the event is confirmed by other reports.
- **Reuters (2023). Maersk schedules dozens of vessels to travel via Suez Canal.** *Malay Mail*, 27 December. [link](https://www.malaymail.com/news/money/2023/12/27/maersk-schedules-dozens-of-vessels-to-travel-via-suez-canal/109485) — **Grade B2**. Summary: After Prosperity Guardian was announced, Maersk briefly planned to send ships back through Suez, a short-lived confidence boost. Reliability: Reuters copy seen as a snippet, and overtaken within a week by Maersk's second halt.
- **Freightos (2024). FBX Index July 2024: market summary.** *Baltic Exchange* (guest column), August. [link](https://www.balticexchange.com/en/news-and-events/news/guest-column/2024/fbx-index-july-24-market-summary.html) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Asia–North Europe container rates peaked at $8,369 per 40-ft box in July 2024, then fell about 60% by October. Reliability: The company that owns the index commenting on its own product, which has a commercial interest in volatility; the date is unconfirmed.
- **Reuters (2024). Egypt's Sisi says regional challenges cost Egypt around $7 bln of Suez Canal revenues in 2024.** *CNBC Africa*, December. [link](https://cnbcafrica.com/wire/655389) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Sisi said Red Sea disruption cost about $7 billion of canal revenue in 2024, a fall of more than 60% on 2023. Reliability: Reuters reporting a presidential claim that differs from the IMF's estimate of about $6 billion; the statement did not name the Houthis.
- **PA Media / Associated Press (2025). Rescuers search for missing crew after Red Sea attack.** *Express & Star*, c. 10 July. [link](https://www.expressandstar.com/world-news/rescuers-search-for-missing-crew-after-red-sea-attack-5684801) — **Grade B2**. Summary: The search for the *Eternity C* crew went on; later reports give four dead, ten rescued and 11 missing, and the US accused the Houthis of taking seafarers. Reliability: Wire copy, but death tolls differ between reports and the kidnapping claim comes from the US, a party to the conflict.
- **Lloyd's List Intelligence (2026). Red Sea Brief: 6 August 2026.** *Lloyd's List Intelligence* blog. [link](https://www.lloydslistintelligence.com/resources/blog/red-sea-brief-6-august-2026) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Bab el-Mandeb traffic fell 24% after the blockade, then levelled off, with about 275 Suez transits in the week to 2 August; some Saudi crude moved via the SUMED pipeline. Reliability: The leading shipping-data firm, though this blog also markets its services; its −24% differs from Infobae's −34%.
- **PBS NewsHour (2026). Houthi strikes on Saudi Arabia show the Yemeni rebels' growing strength.** *PBS NewsHour*. [link](https://www.pbs.org/newshour/world/houthi-strikes-on-saudi-arabia-show-the-yemeni-rebels-growing-strength) — **Grade B2**. Summary: The Houthis resumed attacks on Saudi Arabia and declared a shipping blockade in July, demanding Riyadh lift its own blockade of Yemen; no figure seen. Reliability: A US public broadcaster usually using AP copy; the date is unconfirmed.
- **Times of Israel / AP and Al Jazeera (2025). Yemen's Houthis signal they've stopped attacks on Israel and Red Sea shipping.** *Times of Israel*; *Al Jazeera*, 11 November. [link](https://www.timesofisrael.com/yemens-houthis-signal-theyve-stopped-attacks-on-israel-and-red-sea-shipping/amp/) — **Grade B2**. Summary: The Houthis paused attacks after Gaza ceasefires and restarted when fighting resumed; Al Jazeera cites the IMF's estimate that Egypt lost about $6 billion in canal revenue in 2024. Reliability: Israeli and Qatari outlets that balance each other; snippets only.
- **Lloyd's List and St Louis Fed (2024). Red Sea rerouting is not a rerun of supply chain crisis.** *Lloyd's List*. [link](https://www.lloydslist.com/LL1147914/Red-Sea-rerouting-is-not-a-rerun-of-supply-chain-crisis) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Argues effects would be limited because the Cape detour soaked up spare ships, leaving only 0.6% of container ships idle by late 2024; the Fed explains how local shocks spread. Reliability: The leading shipping paper (paywalled) plus a central-bank blog, both written early in the crisis.
- **Lloyd's List (2024). Red Sea war risk rates see huge jump in wake of Yemen airstrikes.** *Lloyd's List*, c. 16 January. [link](https://www.lloydslist.com/LL1147965/Red-Sea-war-risk-rates-see-huge-jump-in-wake-of-Yemen-airstrikes) — **Grade B2**. Summary: War cover tripled in a week after the US–UK strikes, from 0.3% to 0.75% then 1% of hull value, about 100 times the pre-November 2023 level. Reliability: The leading shipping paper, but the figures are broker quotes and the "100×" depends on the baseline.
- **Reuters (2024). War insurers shrug off Rubymar sinking in Red Sea, rates stable.** *Insurance Journal*, 4 March. [link](https://www.insurancejournal.com/news/international/2024/03/04/763375.htm) — **Grade B2**. Summary: The first ship sunk did not raise premiums, which had already settled at about 1% of ship value, against about 0.5% before. Reliability: Reuters copy using broker and underwriter sources; rates included discounts.
- **S&P Global (2025). Houthi attacks boost insurance rates, ship traffic not yet affected.** *S&P Global Commodity Insights*, 8 July. [link](https://www.spglobal.com/energy/en/news-research/latest-news/shipping/070825-houthi-attacks-boost-insurance-rates-ship-traffic-not-yet-affected) — **Grade B2**. Summary: The attacks on the *Magic Seas* and *Eternity C* raised premiums, but traffic did not fall straight away; no figure seen. Reliability: A respected commodity-pricing service; the events are confirmed by other reports.
- **Reuters (2025). Red Sea insurance premium spikes as Houthi risks return.** *Insurance Journal*, 11 July. [link](https://www.insurancejournal.com/news/international/2025/07/11/831268.htm) — **Grade B2**. Summary: After the two July 2025 sinkings, Red Sea war-risk premiums jumped back to about 1% of hull value. Reliability: Reuters copy using broker sources; the starting level was not seen.
- **Reuters (2026). Three tankers carrying Saudi crude make U-turns in Red Sea after Houthi warning.** *Insurance Journal*, 22 July. [link](https://www.insurancejournal.com/news/international/2026/07/22/878526.htm) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Three tankers with Saudi crude turned back after Houthi threats, cutting trade without a shot being fired. Reliability: Reuters copy based on ship-tracking; a small sample.
- **Reuters (2026). War risk insurance costs surge for southern Red Sea voyages after Houthi attacks.** *Insurance Journal*, 23 July. [link](https://www.insurancejournal.com/news/international/2026/07/23/878788.htm) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Southern Red Sea premiums passed 1%, with Saudi-linked ships paying up to 3%, while Jeddah and Yanbu were still about 0.1%. Reliability: Reuters copy of indicative broker quotes, not prices actually paid.
- **Reuters (2026). Update: three killed in attack on ship in Red Sea; container ship hit by missile: sources.** *Insurance Journal*, 11 August. [link](https://www.insurancejournal.com/news/international/2026/08/11/881026.htm) — **Grade B2**. Summary: One attack killed three people and a missile hit a container ship, showing the 2026 campaign went beyond Saudi tankers. Reliability: An early Reuters "update" story whose details may have changed.
- **Reuters (2026). Saudi oil export strategy hits new hurdle as Red Sea insurance costs soar.** *Insurance Journal*, 25 September. [link](https://www.insurancejournal.com/news/international/2026/09/25/886769.htm) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Saudi-linked tankers at Yanbu paid about 3% (up to 7% at Jizan); a seven-day Yanbu voyage cost about $3 million in premium against at least $100,000 before. Reliability: Reuters copy using indicative broker estimates; backs up the CSIS warning about the pipeline.
- **Lloyd's List (2026). Chinese operators lead surge in Red Sea and Suez vehicle carrier transits.** *Lloyd's List*, c. mid-October. [link](https://www.lloydslist.com/LL1158714/Chinese-operators-lead-surge-in-Red-Sea-and-Suez-vehicle-carrier-transits) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Mostly Chinese car carriers returned to the Red Sea, with 144 Bab el-Mandeb transits in Q3 2026, up almost 150% year on year. Reliability: The leading shipping paper, but a small vessel segment and commercial data.
- **Lloyd's List (2026). VLCC operators rethink Red Sea voyages amid fresh Houthi blockade.** *Lloyd's List*, July. [link](https://www.lloydslist.com/LL1157928/VLCC-operators-rethink-Red-Sea-voyages-amid-fresh-Houthi-blockade) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Tanker owners reconsidered Red Sea voyages after the 20 July ban on Saudi ports, with Saudi-owned fleets expected to be first targets; no figure seen. Reliability: Specialist shipping press, paywalled; snippet only.
- **Lloyd's List (2026). Red Sea traffic holds firm despite Houthi blockade as UN warns Yemen on brink of renewed war.** *Lloyd's List*, c. late September. [link](https://www.lloydslist.com/LL1158196/Red-Sea-traffic-holds-firm-despite-Houthi-blockade-as-UN-warns-Yemen-on-brink-of-renewed-war) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Apart from Saudi-linked tankers, traffic held up; mainstream tanker transits fell 42% in the blockade's first week. Reliability: The leading shipping paper, paywalled; the date is approximate.
- **Lloyd's List Intelligence (2026). Red Sea Brief – 1 October 2026.** *Lloyd's List Intelligence* blog. [link](https://www.lloydslistintelligence.com/resources/blog/red-sea-brief-1-october-2026) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Tankers that had gone "dark" near Yanbu reappeared laden, and no attacks had been reported since a Bahri supertanker was hit on 24 August. Reliability: A leading shipping-data firm's own commercial brief; satellite images back its claims.
- **CNBC (2026). Houthis deploy missiles and drones to attack ships in southern Red Sea, naval group says.** *CNBC*, 22 July. [link](https://www.cnbc.com/2026/07/22/houthis-red-sea-bab-el-mandeb-saudi-oil-iran.html) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Yanbu exports through Bab el-Mandeb had surged to 3.5 million barrels a day in June (Kpler), and the naval information centre JMIC rated the threat "moderate". Reliability: A major US financial broadcaster relaying data-firm and naval sources; snippet only.
- **Reuters (2024). India's exporters struggle as Red Sea crisis favours rivals.** *BusinessLive*, 16 February. [link](https://www.businesslive.co.za/bd/world/asia/2024-02-16-indias-exporters-struggle-as-red-sea-crisis-favours-rivals/) — **Grade B2**. Summary: About 95% of India-origin ships were rerouted via the Cape, and the Tirupur textile hub ran at about a third of capacity. Reliability: Reuters copy in a South African paper; seen as a snippet.
- **Reuters (2026). Yemen's Houthis consider imposing fees on ships sailing through Red Sea: sources.** *Insurance Journal*, 29 July. [link](https://www.insurancejournal.com/news/international/2026/07/29/879298.htm) — **Grade B3**. Summary: Unnamed sources said the Houthis were weighing a toll on Red Sea shipping, echoing Iran's Hormuz scheme; no figure. Reliability: Reuters, but based on anonymous sources about an intention the Houthis later denied.
- **S&P Global (2024). Rubymar sinking highlights shipping risks, supports tanker and insurance rates.** *S&P Global Commodity Insights*, 4 March. [link](https://www.spglobal.com/energy/en/news-research/latest-news/shipping/030424-rubymar-sinking-highlights-shipping-risks-supports-tanker-and-insurance-rates) — **Grade B6**. Summary: Says the sinking held up tanker and insurance rates; separate S&P coverage put 2024 Red Sea premiums mostly at 0.4–0.7%, rarely 1%. Reliability: A respected pricing service, but no figure was visible in this article's snippet.
- **gCaptain (2024). Maersk halts Red Sea voyages "until further notice".** *gCaptain*, c. January. [link](https://gcaptain.com/maersk-pausing-red-sea-transits-until-further-notice/) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Maersk paused on 15 December 2023, briefly restarted under Prosperity Guardian, then stopped indefinitely after the 31 December attack, showing the coalition did not restore confidence. Reliability: Respected maritime trade press with dates confirmed elsewhere; Maersk's statements are corporate messaging.
- **Air & Space Forces Magazine (2023). US, allies take on over 100 attacks by Houthis with new "Operation Prosperity Guardian".** *Air & Space Forces Magazine*, 19 December. [link](https://www.airandspaceforces.com/daily/20231219/) — **Grade C2**. Summary: The Pentagon counted more than 100 drone and missile attacks on 10 merchant ships linked to over 35 nations, while partners had not said what ships they would send. Reliability: Published by the US Air Force Association with a pro-military angle, relying on one official source.
- **ITLN (2024). Drewry index up 61%, Maersk to divert all vessels.** *India Transport & Logistics News*, January. [link](https://itln.in/shipping/drewry-index-up-61-red-sea-attacks-continue-1350816) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Drewry's container index jumped 61% in two weeks to 4 January 2024; Shanghai–Rotterdam rose 115% to $3,577 per 40-ft box. Reliability: Indian trade press reporting Drewry's data second-hand; cite Drewry as the data owner.
- **Seatrade Maritime News (2024). Asia–Europe spot container rates up over 100% due to Red Sea terror.** *Seatrade Maritime*, January. [link](https://www.seatrade-maritime.com/containers/asia-europe-spot-container-rates-up-over-100-due-to-red-sea-terror) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Spot rates on Asia–Europe lanes more than doubled within weeks of the diversions starting. Reliability: Good UK trade press, but no detailed figures were seen and the headline uses loaded wording ("terror").
- **Al-Monitor (2024). Sisi de Egipto: los ingresos del canal de Suez cayeron hasta un 50% por los ataques.** *Al-Monitor* (Spanish edition), February. [link](https://www.al-monitor.com/es/originals/2024/02/sisi-de-egipto-los-ingresos-del-canal-de-suez-cayeron-hasta-un-50-por-los-ataques) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Sisi said canal revenue fell 40–50% in early 2024; the canal chief reported traffic down 30% and dollar revenue down 40% for 1–11 January. Reliability: A Middle East news site reporting Egyptian government figures, so check them against Canal Authority data.
- **Sourcing Journal (2026). Shipping's Suez comeback gathers steam despite Houthi advance.** *WWD / Sourcing Journal*. [link](https://wwd.com/sourcing-journal/logistics/red-sea-return-cosco-oocl-houthis-bab-el-mandeb-suez-canal-strait-of-hormuz-1239231381/) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Cosco and OOCL joined CMA CGM in resuming some Red Sea services (Linerlytica data), but volumes stayed well below pre-crisis levels. Reliability: Retail supply-chain trade press; no figures seen and the date is unconfirmed.
- **Forces News (c. 2024–25). Red Sea trade declines, Houthis grow strength despite Western airstrikes.** *Forces News* (citing IISS). [link](https://www.forcesnews.com/middle-east/red-sea-trade-declines-houthis-grow-strength-despite-western-airstrikes) — **Grade C2**. Summary: The naval operation "successfully prevented the loss of civilian lives" but did not deter the Houthis or bring traffic back to pre-2023 levels. Reliability: A British forces broadcaster passing on IISS research second-hand; the date is unconfirmed.
- **Al Arabiya and Breaking Defense (2023–24). US courts China to join anti-Houthi naval task force / Purported Houthi strike on Chinese vessel likely a mistake.** *Al Arabiya*, 20 December 2023; *Breaking Defense*, March 2024. [link](https://english.alarabiya.net/News/gulf/2023/12/20/US-courts-China-to-join-anti-Houthi-naval-task-force) — **Grade C2**. Summary: China joined neither Prosperity Guardian nor Aspides, yet the Houthis hit a Chinese ship in March 2024 despite promising to spare them. Reliability: A Saudi-owned channel (Saudi Arabia fights in Yemen) and US defence trade press; snippets only.
- **Seatrade Maritime News (2025). Eternity C sinks after Houthi attack in Red Sea, death toll rises.** *Seatrade Maritime*, c. 9 July. [link](https://www.seatrade-maritime.com/security/eternity-c-red-sea-attack-death-toll-rises-as-ship-reported-sunk) — **Grade C2**. Summary: The bulker sank about 51 nautical miles west of Hodeidah after sea-drone and grenade attacks, with at least four of the 25 aboard killed. Reliability: UK trade press citing UKMTO, but casualty figures vary between reports (4 or 9 dead).
- **Splash247 (2025). Suez traffic shows first signs of recovery.** *Splash247* (Clarksons data), c. late November. [link](https://splash247.com/suez-traffic-shows-first-signs-of-recovery/) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Weekly Suez transits rose to about 269 in November 2025 but stayed about half the 495–500 a week seen before the crisis. Reliability: Trade press using commercial data from a shipbroker; the date is approximate.
- **Container News (2026). Suez Canal traffic still 60% below pre-attack levels 100 days on.** *Container News* (BIMCO data), c. 7 January. [link](https://container-news.com/suez-canal-traffic-still-60-below-pre-attack-levels-100-days-on) — **Grade C2**. Summary: A hundred days after the last attack, Suez transits were still about 60% below 2023, and container-ship transits were down 86% in Q4 2025. Reliability: Trade press reporting a shipowners' association whose members have mixed interests; attack counts differ between sources.
- **gCaptain (n.d.). How tiny Djibouti said no to the U.S. over Houthi Red Sea attacks.** *gCaptain*. [link](https://gcaptain.com/how-tiny-djibouti-said-no-to-the-u-s-over-houthi-red-sea-attacks/) — **Grade C2** (graded here). Summary: Djibouti refused US use of its base for strikes on the Houthis but let EU Aspides ships dock and refuel; no figure. Reliability: Respected maritime trade press, consistent with *Africa Confidential*; seen in a snippet only and undated.
- **Maritime Executive (2026). China continues Gulf of Aden security mission by dispatching 49th fleet.** *The Maritime Executive*, August. [link](https://maritime-executive.com/article/china-continues-gulf-of-aden-security-mission-by-dispatching-49th-fleet) — **Grade C2** (graded here). Summary: China's navy has run anti-piracy escort task forces in the Gulf of Aden since December 2008, now its 49th, guarding mainly Chinese ships outside the Western coalitions. Reliability: US maritime trade press, consistent with Xinhua reports in the source file; seen as background only.
- **gCaptain (2026). Red Sea corridor slips back into crisis as Houthi threats resurface.** *gCaptain*. [link](https://gcaptain.com/red-sea-corridor-slips-back-into-crisis-as-houthi-threats-resurface/) — **Grade C3**. Summary: Reports the 2026 return of Houthi risk; a blog citing Reuters says war-risk premiums rose from about 0.3% to 0.75% of hull value after 20 July. Reliability: Reliable trade press, but the premium figure is second-hand and the date is unconfirmed.
- **The Loadstar (2026). Seven MSC box ships "go dark" as they resume Suez canal transits.** *The Loadstar*, c. August. [link](https://theloadstar.com/seven-msc-box-ships-go-dark-as-they-resume-suez-canal-transits/) — **Grade C3**. Summary: MSC sent seven ships through Suez with transponders off to avoid being targeted; Canal Authority data later showed container tonnage up 54.2% year on year in January–August 2026. Reliability: Trade press relying on a commercial analytics firm's claim; the date is unknown.
- **The Washington Post (2026). How the Houthi militia weaponized shipping in the Red Sea [opinion].** *The Washington Post*, 17 August. [link](https://www.washingtonpost.com/opinions/2026/08/17/how-houthi-militia-weaponized-shipping-red-sea/) — **Grade C3**. Summary: Argues Saudi tankers rerouted north to Yanbu, only for Houthi attacks to reach them there, so the bypass failed; no figure. Reliability: An opinion piece in a major paper; the headline comes from the URL.
- **Al Jazeera (2026). Can the Houthis close the Red Sea after seizing the Yemen coast?** *Al Jazeera*, 11 September. [link](https://www.aljazeera.com/news/2026/9/11/can-the-houthis-close-the-red-sea-after-seizing-the-yemen-coast) — **Grade C3**. Summary: Explains that the Houthis now have much firmer control of Bab el-Mandeb after seizing the coast; no figure seen. Reliability: A Qatari state-funded network's explainer seen as a snippet; control of the strait is contested.
- **The Maritime Executive (c. 2024). To safely transit the Red Sea, shadow a Chinese ship [editorial].** *The Maritime Executive*. [link](https://www.maritime-executive.com/editorials/to-safely-transit-the-red-sea-shadow-a-chinese-ship) — **Grade C4**. Summary: A provocative call for ships to shadow Chinese vessels and to "exact a cost" on Chinese "opportunism"; no figure. Reliability: An openly anti-China opinion piece whose premise was undercut by the March 2024 strike on a Chinese ship, so it is not evidence.
- **Hellenic Shipping News (2024). Drewry: World Container Index up 23% last week.** *Hellenic Shipping News*, c. 19 January. [link](https://www.hellenicshippingnews.com/drewry-world-container-index-up-23-last-week/) — **Grade D1**. Summary: Drewry's index hit $3,777 per 40-ft box, 166% above the 2019 average of $1,420 and its highest since October 2022. Reliability: A repost of Drewry's release with no original reporting, though the figures are confirmed elsewhere; cite Drewry itself.

### Regional press (Egypt, Yemen, Israel, the Horn of Africa and others)

- **Reuters (2023). Israel's Eilat Port has 85% drop in activity amid Red Sea Houthi attacks.** *Jerusalem Post* (Israel), 21 December. [link](https://www.jpost.com/middle-east/article-779002) — **Grade B1**. Summary: Eilat's chief executive said activity had fallen 85% since the Houthis began attacking ships; the port mainly handles car imports and potash exports. Reliability: Reuters copy in a free Israeli paper, but the CEO was lobbying for state aid, so treat it as an upper estimate.
- **Reuters (2026). Shipping companies divert vessels around Cape of Good Hope after strikes on Iran.** *Daily Maverick* (South Africa), 1 March. [link](https://www.dailymaverick.co.za/article/2026-03-01-shipping-companies-divert-vessels-around-cape-of-good-hope-after-strikes-on-iran/) — **Grade B1**. Summary: Maersk, Hapag-Lloyd and CMA CGM rerouted round Africa days after Maersk had started returning to Suez, showing how fragile the recovery was. Reliability: Reuters copy in an independent South African paper, confirmed by Egyptian reports of Maersk's renewed pause.
- **Reuters (2026). Huthi blockade threat raises risk of dual energy chokepoint crisis, Kpler says.** *Egypt Independent* (Egypt), c. late July. [link](https://www.egyptindependent.com/huthi-blockade-threat-raises-risk-of-dual-energy-chokepoint-crisis-kpler-says/) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Kpler counted about 41 ships a day through Bab el-Mandeb in mid-July 2026, 43% below the 2023 peak of 72, as Riyadh relied on Red Sea ports to bypass Hormuz. Reliability: Wire copy using independent ship-tracking data, carried by a private Egyptian paper.
- **Reuters (2026). China in touch with Yemen's Houthis to allow ships to sail through Red Sea, sources say.** *Arab News* (Saudi Arabia), c. July–August. [link](https://www.arabnews.com/node/2652630/world) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Beijing reportedly negotiated directly with the Houthis for safe passage of Chinese tankers carrying Saudi crude from Yanbu; no figure. Reliability: Reuters copy carried by a Saudi state-aligned paper; based on unnamed sources.
- **AP/AFP (2026). Houthis seize key Red Sea islands, complete takeover of vital Bab el-Mandeb shipping lane.** *Times of Israel* (Israel), c. mid-September. [link](https://www.timesofisrael.com/houthis-seize-key-red-sea-island-complete-takeover-of-vital-bab-al-mandab-shipping-lane/) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Reports the Houthi capture of the Hanish islands and Perim; related Kpler data show 32 ships a day in the strait against about 50 before. Reliability: Wire copy in an independent Israeli paper, but Yemen's government later claimed to have retaken areas.
- **Reuters (2026). Saudi Arabia seeks coalition to safeguard Red Sea shipping: sources.** *Daily Sabah* (Turkey), c. July. [link](https://www.dailysabah.com/world/mid-east/saudi-arabia-seeks-coalition-to-safeguard-red-sea-shipping-sources) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Riyadh tried to assemble an international coalition to protect Red Sea shipping after the Houthi blockade; no figure. Reliability: Reuters copy carried by a strongly pro-government Turkish paper; based on unnamed sources.
- **Ahram Online (2023). Yemen's Houthis hijacked an Israeli-owned vessel in the Red Sea.** *Ahram Online* (Egypt), November. [link](https://english.ahram.org.eg/News/512512.aspx) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Reports the helicopter seizure of the car carrier *Galaxy Leader* near Hodeidah with 25 crew, the start of the Houthi campaign. Reliability: Egyptian state-owned media, with the crew details confirmed by Reuters; Egypt has a stake because canal revenue is at risk.
- **Egypt Independent (2024). Suez Canal revenues decline up to 50% due to Red Sea tensions: Sisi at EGYPES 2024.** *Egypt Independent* (Egypt), c. February. [link](https://www.egyptindependent.com/suez-canal-revenues-decline-up-to-50-due-to-red-sea-tensions-sisi-at-egypes-2024/) — **Grade C1**. Summary: President Sisi said canal revenue had fallen 40–50% since the start of 2024. Reliability: A private paper working under censorship that repeats the presidential line uncritically, though other outlets report the same figure.
- **Daily News Egypt (2024). Suez Canal revenues down by half: Al-Sisi.** *Daily News Egypt* (Egypt), 19 February. [link](https://www.dailynewsegypt.com/2024/02/19/suez-canal-revenues-down-by-half-al-sisi/) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Repeats Sisi's estimate that revenue had halved, against the roughly $10 billion a year the canal used to earn. Reliability: A private Egyptian paper under censorship relaying an official claim confirmed by other outlets.
- **Mada Masr (2023). US to assemble international maritime security force to protect shipping lines after Houthi attacks.** *Mada Masr* (Egypt), 19 December. [link](https://www.madamasr.com/en/2023/12/19/news/u/shipping-lines-reroute-traffic-away-from-suez-canal-due-to-houthi-attacks-us-assembles-international-maritime-security-force/) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Reports Prosperity Guardian being set up as carriers rerouted away from Suez, and notes Egypt did not publicly join. Reliability: Egypt's main independent outlet (blocked inside Egypt); facts confirmed by wire reports.
- **Mada Masr (2025). Leaked: US expects "return" from Egypt after bombing Yemen to secure Suez shipping.** *Mada Masr* (Egypt), 26 March. [link](https://www.madamasr.com/en/2025/03/26/news/politics/leaked-us-expects-return-from-egypt-after-bombing-yemen-to-secure-suez-shipping/) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Based on the leaked "Signal chat", US officials expected Egypt to give something back for US strikes protecting Suez traffic. Reliability: An independent outlet critical of both governments, drawing on a widely reported leak.
- **Times of Israel (2024). Hurt by Houthi blockade, debt-ridden Eilat port seeks aid to help it stay afloat.** *Times of Israel* (Israel), c. July. [link](https://www.timesofisrael.com/debt-ridden-eilat-port-hurt-by-houthi-blockade-seeks-aid-to-help-it-stay-afloat/) — **Grade C1**. Summary: The port faced closure without state help, with about 170 jobs at risk; its CEO told the Knesset it had been inactive for eight months and was "in a state of bankruptcy". Reliability: An independent centrist paper, confirmed by other reports, though the port survived on state aid.
- **Haaretz (2026). Iran-backed Houthis close in on Red Sea gateway in new threat to shipping.** *Haaretz* (Israel), 10 September. [link](https://www.haaretz.com/middle-east-news/2026-09-10/ty-article/.premium/iran-backed-houthis-close-in-on-bab-el-mandeb-strait-in-new-threat-to-shipping/000001a0-8b3a-d532-a9ba-8f3e07270000) — **Grade C1**. Summary: The Houthis seized the port of Mokha, according to four government military sources, a shift that could threaten the strait itself. Reliability: An independent left-liberal paper (paywalled); the capture is confirmed by other reports.
- **Garowe Online (2023). Fears that pirates are returning to seas off Somalia.** *Garowe Online* (Somalia), c. late 2023. [link](https://www.garoweonline.com/en/news/somalia/fears-that-pirates-are-returning-to-seas-off-somalia) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Reports the first signs of a piracy revival, including dhow hijackings, as navies moved north to counter the Houthis. Reliability: A private Puntland-focused site shaped by clan politics; the revival is confirmed by later hijackings.
- **Garowe Online (2024). Somali pirates confirm taking $5 million ransom to release Bangladeshi ship.** *Garowe Online* (Somalia), c. April. [link](https://www.garoweonline.com/en/news/somalia/somali-pirates-confirm-taking-5-million-ransom-to-release-bangladeshi-ship) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Pirates said they received $5 million for the *MV Abdullah* and its 23 crew; Reuters reported a UK-based insurer arranged the payment. Reliability: A private Somali site quoting the pirates themselves, backed by Reuters reporting.
- **Daily Sabah (2023). Attacks in Red Sea force rerouting of vessels, disrupt supply chains.** *Daily Sabah* (Turkey), c. December. [link](https://www.dailysabah.com/business/transportation/attacks-in-red-sea-force-rerouting-of-vessels-disrupt-supply-chains) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Maersk, MSC and others rerouted via the Cape, adding a week or more to Asia–Europe voyages. Reliability: A strongly pro-government Turkish paper, but the facts are widely confirmed.
- **Egypt Independent (2025/26). "Shipping giants" return to Suez Canal.** *Egypt Independent* (Egypt), c. December 2025–January 2026. [link](https://www.egyptindependent.com/shipping-giants-return-to-suez-canal/) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Carriers including CMA CGM resumed Suez transits after the Gaza peace deal, and a war-risk insurance discount was agreed except for Israel-bound ships. Reliability: A private paper under censorship with upbeat, official-sourced coverage.
- **DNE Africa (n.d.). Egypt loses $7 billion of Suez Canal revenues as Red Sea tensions disrupt traffic.** *Daily News Egypt*. [link](https://africa.dailynewsegypt.com/egypt-loses-7-billion-of-suez-canal-revenue-as-red-sea-tensions-disrupt-traffic/) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Reports the presidency's estimate of about $7 billion lost in 2024, a fall of more than 60%; Maersk said its own transits fell 66%. Reliability: A state-aligned outlet promoting government deals.
- **Saba (2025). Remarks by Al-Sayyid Abdul Malik Badruddin Al-Houthi on decision to ban navigation of Israeli ships in declared areas.** *Saba* (Houthi-run, Yemen), c. March. [link](https://www.saba.ye/en/news3449986.htm) — **Grade D2**. Summary: The Houthi leader announces the restart of the ban on Israel-linked shipping after the Gaza ceasefire broke down. Reliability: Houthi state media; cite it only as a statement of intent.
- **Arab News (2026). Saudi Arabia strongly condemns claim that Houthis have imposed maritime blockade on Kingdom.** *Arab News* (Saudi Arabia), 20 July. [link](https://www.arabnews.com/node/2651670/saudi-arabia) — **Grade D2**. Summary: The Saudi Foreign Ministry called the blockade illegal and vowed "all necessary measures" to protect ships. Reliability: A state-aligned paper giving its own government's line, good for what Riyadh said, not what happened.
- **Arab News (2026). Analysis: Why the Houthis are miscalculating this time.** *Arab News* (Saudi Arabia), c. late July. [link](https://www.arabnews.com/node/2651943/middle-east) — **Grade D2**. Summary: Argues the new threat covers any ship using Saudi ports; four Saudi crude tankers turned back and two Saudi-flagged tankers were hit two days after the declaration. Reliability: Pro-government analysis about Saudi Arabia's own conflict.
- **Arab News (2026). Allies unite behind Saudi Arabia as Houthis step up attacks.** *Arab News* (Saudi Arabia), c. October. [link](https://www.arabnews.com/middle-east/allies-unite-behind-saudi-arabia-as-houthis-step-up-attacks-3003050) — **Grade D2**. Summary: Reports regional support for Riyadh as attacks spread, alongside the Saudi–Turkey–Pakistan defence pact of 7 August 2026; no figure. Reliability: A state-aligned paper on its own government's diplomacy.
- **La Nation (2025). Exercice Red Wave: les forces navales saoudiennes, en collaboration avec des pays de la région dont Djibouti, lancent des manœuvres de grande envergure dans la mer Rouge.** *La Nation* (Djibouti), c. November. [link](https://www.lanation.dj/exercice-red-wave-les-forces-navales-saoudiennes-en-collaboration-avec-des-pays-de-la-region-dont-djibouti-lancent-des-manoeuvres-de-grande-envergure-dans-la-mer-rouge/) — **Grade D2**. Summary: Djibouti joined Saudi-led "Red Wave" naval drills with other Red Sea coastal states; no figure. Reliability: Djibouti's state-owned daily under tight press control, reporting its own government.
- **La Nation (2026). African Maritime Forces Summit 2026: Djibouti affirme son rôle de sentinelle maritime.** *La Nation* (Djibouti), c. July. [link](https://www.lanation.dj/african-maritime-forces-summit-amfs-2026-djibouti-affirme-son-role-de-sentinelle-maritime-au-coeur-des-enjeux-securitaires-africains/) — **Grade D2**. Summary: Djibouti presents itself as guardian of Bab el-Mandeb, protecting ports with its navy and coast guard, without naming the Houthis. Reliability: State-owned media promoting the government's image.
- **Shabait (2026). El-Alamein Joint Declaration: the Quadrilateral Consultative Summit between Egypt, Sudan, Eritrea and Somalia.** *Shabait* (Eritrea), 4 October. [link](https://shabait.com/2026/10/04/el-alamein-joint-declaration-the-quadrilateral-consultative-summit-between-egypt-sudan-eritrea-and-somalia/) — **Grade D2**. Summary: The four states declared Red Sea security the "exclusive responsibility of the littoral states", rejecting outside navies and Ethiopia. Reliability: Eritrea's state information ministry (last in the world for press freedom); good as the text of the states' position.
- **Shabait (2026). Minister Osman Saleh holds talks with his Egyptian counterpart.** *Shabait* (Eritrea), 19 July. [link](https://shabait.com/2026/07/19/minister-osman-saleh-holds-talks-with-his-egyptian-counterpart/) — **Grade D2**. Summary: Eritrea and Egypt said Red Sea security belongs to coastal states and discussed reviving the Saudi-based Red Sea Council. Reliability: Eritrean state media reporting its own government.
- **Jordan Times (2026). Jordan condemns Houthi claim that Saudi Arabia is besieging Yemen.** *Jordan Times* (Jordan), c. 21 July. [link](https://jordantimes.com/news/local/jordan-condemns-houthi-claim-that-saudi-arabia-is-besieging-yemen) — **Grade D2**. Summary: Amman called the Houthi claims baseless and stood with Riyadh. Reliability: A semi-official paper reporting its own government's position.
- **Hürriyet Daily News (2026). Türkiye condemns Houthi threat against Saudi Arabia.** *Hürriyet Daily News* (Turkey), c. 21 July. [link](https://www.hurriyetdailynews.com/turkiye-condemns-houthi-threat-against-saudi-arabia-224693) — **Grade D2**. Summary: Turkey's Foreign Ministry called for an end to threats to Red Sea security, weeks before signing a defence pact with Saudi Arabia and Pakistan. Reliability: A pro-government paper reporting its own government.
- **Ahram Online (2024). Cape of Good Hope route unsustainable as it lacks necessary services: Suez Canal chairman.** *Ahram Online* (Egypt), c. mid-2024. [link](https://english.ahram.org.eg/NewsContent/1/1235/525398/Egypt/Urban--Transport/Cape-of-Good-Hope-route-unsustainable-as-it-lacks-.aspx) — **Grade D3**. Summary: The canal chief argued the Cape lacks the ports and bunkering of the Suez route, so diversions cannot last; no figure. Reliability: A self-interested sales pitch in state-owned media, partly backed by South African reporting.
- **Yemen Online (2026). Houthis claim blocking 48 Saudi oil tankers, launching strikes inside Kingdom.** *Yemen Online* (Yemen). [link](https://yemenonline.info/special-reports/13516) — **Grade D3**. Summary: Relays the Houthi claim to have turned back 48 Saudi tankers under the blockade. Reliability: A partisan repost site passing on an unverified Houthi claim.
- **La Nation (n.d.). Le parcours des ports africains à la lumière de la crise de la mer Rouge: les ports de Djibouti comme modèle d'adaptation.** *La Nation* (Djibouti). [link](https://www.lanation.dj/le-parcours-des-ports-africains-a-la-lumiere-de-la-crise-de-la-mer-rouge-etude-de-cas-les-ports-de-djibouti-comme-modele-dadaptation-et-dexploitation-des-opportunites/) — **Grade D3**. Summary: Presents Djibouti's ports as gaining transshipment traffic from the crisis; foreign reports cite up to a 10% container rise at Doraleh. Reliability: An undated promotional feature in state-owned media.
- **La Nation (n.d.). Garde-Côtes Djiboutiennes: les anges gardiens d'un espace maritime névralgique.** *La Nation* (Djibouti). [link](https://www.lanation.dj/garde-cotes-djiboutiennes-les-anges-gardiens-dun-espace-maritime-nevralgique/) — **Grade D3**. Summary: Profiles the coast guard's anti-piracy, port-protection and rescue work, using patrol boats supplied by Japan; no figure. Reliability: An undated promotional feature in state-owned media.
- **Shabait (2025). "No force substitutes the littoral states of the Red Sea": President Isaias Afwerki.** *Shabait* (Eritrea), 12 November. [link](https://shabait.com/2025/11/12/no-force-substitutes-the-littoral-states-of-the-red-sea-president-isaias-afwerki/) — **Grade D3**. Summary: Isaias rejects foreign bases and outside naval missions unless the UN Security Council approves them, and alleges foreign base-building on Red Sea islands. Reliability: Eritrean state propaganda; the base claims are unverified.
- **Daily Sabah (n.d.). Who really won Red Sea crisis? [op-ed].** *Daily Sabah* (Turkey). [link](https://www.dailysabah.com/opinion/op-ed/who-really-won-red-sea-crisis) — **Grade D3**. Summary: Weighs winners and losers of the crisis and promotes the Iraq–Turkey "Development Road" as a chokepoint-free alternative; no figure. Reliability: Pro-government advocacy for a state project, so treat it as opinion.
- **Saba (c. 2024–25). America's withdrawal from Red Sea confirms fall of Washington's superpower myth.** *Saba* (Houthi-run, Yemen). [link](https://www.saba.ye/en/news3326440.htm) — **Grade D4**. Summary: Claims the US and its coalition "officially withdrew" warships from the Red Sea, presented as a Houthi victory. Reliability: Houthi propaganda that conflicts with other reporting; use it only as evidence of the Houthi narrative.
- **Jordan Times (n.d.). Ministry explores alternatives for goods' transportation through Red Sea.** *Jordan Times* (Jordan). [link](https://jordantimes.com/news/local/ministry-explores-alternatives-goods%E2%80%99-transportation-through-red-sea) — **Grade D4**. Summary: The transport minister said diversions had minimal effect on Jordan's trade and pointed to land and sea links via Egyptian ports. Reliability: Official reassurance in a semi-official paper that conflicts with Aqaba's own port data.

### World press

- **ANSA (2026). Medio Oriente: cos'è la missione Aspides, lo scudo europeo sulle rotte del Mar Rosso.** *ANSA* (Italy), 15 March. [link](https://www.ansa.it/sito/notizie/mondo/2026/03/15/medio-oriente-cose-la-missione-aspides-lo-scudo-europeo-sulle-rotte-del_49fbb2cc-af81-4448-aa21-32f858a5b251.html) — **Grade B1**. Summary: Explains that Aspides, launched in February 2024, is defensive only, covers the Red Sea to the Gulf, and runs until at least March 2027. Reliability: Italy's main news agency, a publishers' cooperative; the facts match the EU legal texts.
- **ANSA (2024). Navi, aerei e uomini a difesa del Mar Rosso.** *ANSA* (Italy), 5 March. [link](https://www.ansa.it/sito/notizie/mondo/2024/03/05/navi-aerei-e-uomini-a-difesa-del-mar-rosso_7a1b1cc1-face-4361-a549-fe4be5d95767.html) — **Grade B2**. Summary: An overview of the ships, aircraft and personnel committed to the new EU Aspides mission and other Red Sea operations; no figure seen. Reliability: Italy's main news agency (Italy is a leading Aspides contributor); seen as a snippet.
- **ANSA (2026). Kallas: "Inaccettabile l'attacco degli Houthi contro petroliera nel Mar Rosso".** *ANSA* (Italy), 23 July. [link](https://www.ansa.it/europa/notizie/rubriche/altrenews/2026/07/23/kallas-inaccettabile-lattacco-degli-houthi-contro-petroliera-nel-mar-rosso_1c955310-0032-4c56-a5d1-7797c95375c5.html) — **Grade B2**. Summary: The EU foreign-policy chief condemned the first Houthi attacks after the 20 July blockade and called for free navigation in both Hormuz and the Red Sea. Reliability: Italy's main agency reporting an official EU statement.
- **ANSA (2026). Kallas: "Attacchi degli Houthi inaccettabili, Aspides innalza l'allerta".** *ANSA* (Italy), 18 September. [link](https://www.ansa.it/europa/notizie/rubriche/altrenews/2026/09/18/-kallas-attacchi-degli-houthi-inaccettabili-aspides-innalza-lallerta-_3b2d7ec1-5b02-4ee5-8862-18206cd51620.html) — **Grade B2**. Summary: Aspides raised its alert level; Italy asked for a stronger mission, and Kallas reportedly said more than ten ships were needed. Reliability: Italy's main agency; the "ten ships" quote comes from a related report.
- **Moncloa.com, Metro Puerto Rico and GBM/Reuters (2026). Los hutíes declaran un bloqueo naval contra Arabia Saudí / Los hutíes dicen que lanzaron misiles contra un petrolero saudita.** 21 and 28 July. [link](https://gbm.com/media/noticia/los-huties-de-yemen-dicen-que-lanzaron-misiles-contra-un-petrolero-saudita-en-el-mar-rojo/) — **Grade B2**. Summary: The 20 July 2026 blockade answered Saudi Arabia's blockade of Houthi ports and a strike on Sanaa airport; the Houthis fired missiles at a Saudi tanker on 28 July. Reliability: Reuters carries the key facts; Moncloa.com is a weak small site.
- **La Nación (2026). Los hutíes anuncian un bloqueo marítimo contra Arabia Saudita y elevan el riesgo para el comercio global.** *La Nación* (Argentina), 20 July. [link](https://www.lanacion.com.ar/el-mundo/los-huties-anuncian-un-bloqueo-maritimo-contra-arabia-saudita-y-elevan-el-riesgo-para-el-comercio-nid20072026/) — **Grade C1**. Summary: A Spanish-language report of the Houthi blockade; the spokesman did not say how or where it would be enforced. Reliability: A major Argentine daily, probably using agency copy, adding no new facts beyond AP.
- **Prensa Latina (2024). Crisis en Mar Rojo provocó caída de ingresos de canal de Suez egipcio.** *Prensa Latina* (Cuba), 19 July. [link](https://www.prensa-latina.cu/2024/07/19/crisis-en-mar-rojo-provoco-caida-de-ingresos-de-canal-de-suez-egipcio-2/) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Canal revenue fell to $7.2 billion in 2023/24 from $9.4 billion, and transits from 25,911 to 20,148; Egypt raised fees by 5–15% to compensate. Reliability: A Cuban state agency with no stake in the canal; the figures come from the Canal Authority and are confirmed elsewhere.
- **Última Hora (2023). España descarta participar en la operación para velar por la seguridad del mar Rojo.** *Última Hora* (Spain), 19 December. [link](https://www.ultimahora.es/noticias/nacional/2023/12/19/2071283/espana-descarta-participar-operacion-para-velar-por-seguridad-mar-rojo.html) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Spain's Defence Ministry said it would not join the US-led Prosperity Guardian; minister Robles said Spain only joins EU, NATO or UN missions. Reliability: A Balearic regional daily relaying ministry statements, confirmed by other outlets.
- **Muñoz, I. (2024). Robles asegura que la misión de EEUU en el mar Rojo no está definida.** *El Español* (Spain), 4 January. [link](https://www.elespanol.com/espana/politica/20240104/robles-asegura-mision-eeuu-mar-rojo-no-definida-espana-participara/822418061_0.amp.html) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Robles said "in no case did the United States ask Spain's authorisation" and that Spain decides where it takes part. Reliability: A centre-right digital paper critical of the government; direct quotes, seen as a snippet.
- **The Objective (2024). Aspides: la misión de la UE en el mar Rojo.** *The Objective* (Spain), 31 January. [link](https://theobjective.com/internacional/2024-01-31/aspides-mision-ue-mar-rojo/) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Josep Borrell confirmed Spain would not join Aspides, whose ships came from France, Germany, Greece and Italy, with headquarters at Larissa in Greece. Reliability: A right-leaning outlet that may frame non-participation critically; facts confirmed by EFE and the EU.
- **Valencia Plaza (2024). El Puerto de València recibe el primer gran buque desviado por la situación en el mar Rojo.** *Valencia Plaza* (Spain). [link](https://valenciaplaza.com/valenciaplaza/el-puerto-de-valencia-recibe-el-primer-gran-buque-desviado-por-la-situacion-en-el-mar-rojo) — **Grade C1**. Summary: The *MSC Isabella* was the first big ship from Asia to reach Valencia via the Cape; local exporters complained of delays and higher freight. - **South China Morning Post (2024). Chinese-owned tanker hit by Houthi missiles in Red Sea days after China told its ships are safe.** *SCMP* (Hong Kong), c. March. [link](https://www.scmp.com/news/world/middle-east/article/3256505/chinese-owned-tanker-hit-houthi-missiles-red-sea-days-after-china-told-its-ships-are-safe) — **Grade C1**. Summary: The Houthis hit the Chinese-owned tanker *Huang Pu* despite assuring Beijing its ships were safe, showing the limits of separate deals. Reliability: An Alibaba-owned paper more willing than mainland media to report awkward facts; confirmed by other reports.
- **Business Standard (2024). Indian Navy ship, commandos force pirates to surrender, rescue MV Ruen crew.** *Business Standard* (India), 16 March. [link](https://www.business-standard.com/external-affairs-defence-security/news/indian-navy-commandos-launch-op-to-rescue-mv-ruen-crew-from-pirates-report-124031600700_1.html) — **Grade C1**. Summary: Pirates fired on an Indian warship before surrendering, in an operation about 2,400–2,600 km from India. Reliability: A private Indian business paper; the rescue is confirmed by several outlets.
- **Ada Derana (2024). India navy recaptures ship from Somali pirates and rescues crew.** *Ada Derana* (Sri Lanka), March. [link](https://www.adaderana.lk/news/98018/india-navy-recaptures-ship-from-somali-pirates-and-rescues-crew) — **Grade C1**. Summary: A neighbouring country's coverage of India's rescue of the *MV Ruen*; no new figure. Reliability: A private Sri Lankan outlet; the event is widely confirmed but it adds little.
- **Forbes Colombia (2024). Maersk desviará todas sus rutas del mar Rojo por el sur de África durante futuro inmediato.** *Forbes Colombia*, 5 January. [link](https://forbes.co/2024/01/05/actualidad/maersk-desviara-todas-sus-rutas-del-mar-rojo-por-el-sur-de-africa-durante-futuro-inmediato/) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Maersk confirmed all its Red Sea and Gulf of Aden ships would sail via the Cape "for the foreseeable future". Reliability: Spanish-language business news based on agency copy; the exact URL is uncertain.
- **Ara (2023–24). España no participará en la operación en el mar Rojo y propone una misión específica.** *Ara* (Spanish edition). [link](https://es.ara.cat/politica/espana-no-participara-operacion-mar-rojo-propone-mision-especifica_1_4894882.html) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Spain blocked changing Atalanta's mandate to cover the Red Sea and proposed a separate mission, while coalition partner Sumar opposed any involvement. Reliability: A Catalan centre-left paper that may stress coalition tensions; EU details come from unnamed sources.
- **Vivir Ediciones / EFE (2025). [Atalanta commander Ignacio Villanueva on piracy trends].** *Vivir Ediciones* (Spain), June. [link](https://vivirediciones.es/?p=840236) — **Grade C2**. Summary: The Atalanta commander reported 46 pirate attacks in 2024 and a fall in 2025. Reliability: EFE wire copy on a repost site, from one official; cross-check with IMB data.
- **The Objective (2023). El puerto de Algeciras se convierte en punto estratégico tras los ataques en el mar Rojo.** *The Objective* (Spain), 24 December. [link](https://theobjective.com/espana/andalucia/2023-12-24/puerto-algeciras-convierte-punto-estrategico-ataques-mar-rojo/) — **Grade C2**. Summary: As big carriers switched to the Cape, Algeciras gained importance; it puts the Red Sea at about 15% of world sea trade (EFE says 11%). Reliability: A right-leaning digital outlet writing early, before traffic data existed.
- **Shippers.cat (2024). Crónicas logísticas: el conflicto en el canal de Suez y el mar Rojo.** Catalan shippers' council. [link](https://www.shippers.cat/es/cronicas-logisticas-canal-de-suez-mar-rojo/) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Suez revenue fell 50–60% in the first eight months of 2024, and the 8% of world LNG exports using Suez stopped from January 2024. - **Observador.cr (2024). El complejo y costoso desvío de 6.500 km que deben tomar los grandes cargueros para evitar los ataques en el mar Rojo.** *Observador.cr* (Costa Rica). Summary: The Cape detour adds about 6,500 km and up to 14 days; the Canal Authority reported revenue down 40% and traffic down 30% in early 2024. - **The Logistics World / EFE (2024). Los ataques hutíes en mar Rojo caen en abril pero tránsito en el Canal de Suez no remonta.** *The Logistics World*. [link](https://thelogisticsworld.com/actualidad-logistica/los-ataques-huties-en-mar-rojo-caen-en-abril-pero-transito-en-el-canal-de-suez-no-remonta/embed/) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Even when attacks fell in April 2024, Suez traffic did not recover, because perceived risk kept ships away. Reliability: EFE wire copy on a repost site; month-by-month figures were not seen.

Summary: Explains China's refusal to join Prosperity Guardian although its Gulf of Aden escort force was nearby; analysts called it a "free ride". - **South China Morning Post (2024). Great power lite? Why China is reluctant to wade into the Red Sea crisis and join US-led attacks on Houthis.** *SCMP* (Hong Kong), c. January. Summary: Discusses why Beijing avoids military involvement despite its huge trade stake; no figure. Reliability: An Alibaba-owned Hong Kong paper; seen as a snippet.
- **South China Morning Post (2024). Red Sea crisis sees China's brisk business in Africa waver under high shipping costs amid Houthi attacks.** *SCMP* (Hong Kong), c. February. - **South China Morning Post (2026). Iran war: Chinese tanker passes Red Sea choke point despite Houthi blockade.** *SCMP* (Hong Kong). - **South China Morning Post (2026). Iran war escalates as Houthis threaten to sever Saudi oil exports.** *SCMP This Week in Asia* (Hong Kong). - **To Vima (2024). Houthis agree to ensure safe passage of Russian and Chinese ships in Red Sea and Gulf of Aden.** *To Vima* (Greece), January. [link](https://www.tovima.com/politics/houthis-agree-to-ensure-safe-passage-of-russian-and-chinese-ships-in-red-sea-and-gulf-of-aden/amp/) — **Grade C2**. Summary: Reports the Houthi pledge, made to Russia's Izvestia, that Chinese and Russian ships with no Israeli links would be safe, leaving Greek owners exposed. Reliability: A Greek paper owned by a shipowner, which shapes its angle.
- **Infobae (2026). Arabia Saudita condenó el bloqueo marítimo anunciado por los hutíes / Cae el tránsito en el mar Rojo.** *Infobae* (Argentina), 21 and 23 July. [link](https://www.infobae.com/america/mundo/2026/07/21/arabia-saudita-condeno-el-bloqueo-maritimo-anunciado-por-los-huties-y-prometio-proteger-sus-buques-con-todas-las-medidas-necesarias/) — **Grade C3**. Summary: Riyadh condemned the blockade; Bab el-Mandeb transits fell 34% to 29 ships in one day and oil rose above $90 a barrel. Reliability: An aggregator of agency copy; the tracking firm is unnamed and the −34% differs from Lloyd's List's −24%.
- **Infobae (2026). Nueva escalada en el mar Rojo: los rebeldes hutíes lanzaron un ataque con misiles contra dos petroleros de Arabia Saudita.** *Infobae* (Argentina), 5 August. [link](https://www.infobae.com/america/mundo/2026/08/05/nueva-escalada-en-el-mar-rojo-los-rebeldes-huties-lanzaron-un-ataque-con-misiles-contra-dos-petroleros-de-arabia-saudita/) — **Grade C3**. Summary: The Houthis fired missiles at two Saudi tankers and claimed nine attacked since 22 July, as Yanbu had become Saudi Arabia's main oil outlet. Reliability: An aggregator passing on an unverified Houthi claim.
- **Infobae and Moncloa.com (2026). La coalición saudita derribó drones y misiles lanzados por los hutíes / Egipto se compromete a defender a Arabia Saudí.** 16–19 October. [link](https://www.infobae.com/america/mundo/2026/10/16/la-coalicion-saudita-derribo-drones-y-misiles-lanzados-por-los-huties-que-iban-dirigidos-contra-riad-y-el-mar-rojo/) — **Grade C3**. Summary: The war widened, with attacks on Riyadh killing at least 14 according to Saudi authorities, and Egypt pledging to defend Saudi Arabia. Reliability: An aggregator and a minor site; casualty figures come from a party to the conflict and events are very recent.
- **Euronews and Swissinfo/EFE (2026). Los hutíes replican la estrategia de Irán en Hormuz: peaje en el mar Rojo / Hutíes niegan imponer tarifas.** 29–31 July. [link](https://es.euronews.com/2026/07/29/los-huties-replican-la-estrategia-de-iran-en-hormuz-peaje-en-el-mar-rojo) — **Grade C3**. Summary: A Yemeni minister alleged Houthi tolls advised by Iran, which the Houthis denied; Saudi Arabia announced a "Multinational Maritime Defence Alliance" for the Red Sea. Reliability: Pan-European news and EFE wire copy, but the toll story is a hostile minister's allegation.
- **El Universal and Moncloa.com (2026). Los hutíes toman Bab el Mandeb y amenazan el comercio mundial.** *El Universal* (Colombia), 13 September. [link](https://www.eluniversal.com.co/mundo/2026/09/13/los-huties-toman-bab-el-mandeb-y-amenazan-el-comercio-mundial/) — **Grade C3**. Summary: On 11 September 2026 the Houthis seized Perim island in the middle of Bab el-Mandeb after taking Mocha; about 8% of world oil passes the strait. Reliability: A Colombian regional paper using AFP copy; a single wire chain, so verify it.
- **Escudo Digital (2025). España asume el mando de Atalanta con la fragata Numancia tras cinco ataques piratas en diez días.** *Escudo Digital* (Spain). [link](https://www.escudodigital.com/defensa/espana-asume-el-mando-de-atalanta-con-la-fragata-numancia-tras-cinco-ataques-piratas-en-diez-dias.html) — **Grade C4**. Summary: Spain took command of Atalanta after more than five pirate attacks in ten days, linking the piracy revival to ships rerouting because of the Houthis. Reliability: A defence news site whose commander name and date conflict with official sources; check the Spanish Defence Staff first.
- **All India Radio (2024). Indian Navy rescues 17 crew members of hijacked cargo vessel MV Ruen, captures 35 pirates after 40-hour operation.** *newsonair* (India), 17 March. [link](https://www.newsonair.gov.in/indian-navy-rescues-17-crew-members-of-hijacked-cargo-vessel-mv-ruen-captures-35-pirates-after-40-hour-operation) — **Grade D1**. Summary: INS *Kolkata*, drones, aircraft and commandos forced 35 Somali pirates to surrender and freed 17 crew of a ship hijacked in December 2023. Reliability: India's state broadcaster with a celebratory tone; the facts are confirmed elsewhere.
- **Global Times (2023). Red Sea attacks intensify, major global shippers avoid Suez Canal.** *Global Times* (China), December. [link](https://www.globaltimes.cn/page/202312/1303862.shtml) — **Grade D2**. Summary: An early report on carriers abandoning the Suez route, focusing on costs to China and Asia–Europe trade; no figure seen. Reliability: Chinese Communist Party-owned media; factual market reporting here, but use it for China's framing.
- **China Daily (2024). Red Sea crisis mounts pressure on Asia trade.** *China Daily* (China), 31 January. [link](https://www.chinadaily.com.cn/a/202401/31/WS65b9822ea3104efcbdae8c26.html) — **Grade D2**. Summary: Describes rising costs and delays for Asian exporters as ships divert round Africa, casting Asia as the victim; no figure seen. Reliability: A state-owned paper aimed at foreign readers.
- **Xinhua (2024). World Insights: Houthi attacks, U.S. airstrikes escalate Red Sea crisis, disrupt global shipping.** *Xinhua* (China), 1 February. [link](https://english.news.cn/20240201/82a5bacfecde40da925ca8820dc47080/c.html) — **Grade D2**. Summary: Cites UNCTAD data on big carriers switching to the Cape and blames Houthi attacks and US strikes equally for escalation. Reliability: China's official state news agency, using it to frame the US as an escalator.
- **Xinhua (2024). Houthi attacks on Red Sea shipping add to maritime woes, lead to global price hikes: UN.** *Xinhua* (China), 26 January. [link](https://english.news.cn/20240126/543a333102f64b3390e17b562dc74db1/c.html) — **Grade D2**. Summary: Relays the UN/UNCTAD warning that Red Sea disruption is raising global shipping costs and prices. Reliability: A state agency, but here relaying UN data; cite UNCTAD directly.
- **Xinhua (2025). 47 ships reroute to Suez Canal since Feb.: canal chief.** *Xinhua* (China), 24 February. [link](https://english.news.cn/20250224/01a6dc3c3a2f4cc197c4531cec1b0830/c.html) — **Grade D2**. Summary: The canal chief said 47 ships returned to Suez after attacks stopped with the January 2025 Gaza ceasefire; 2024 revenue fell over 60% (about $7 billion). Reliability: Chinese state media choosing facts that support Beijing's "root cause" argument.
- **Global Times (2024). US-led airstrikes on Houthis can't stop Red Sea crisis "as root cause in Gaza remained unsolved".** *Global Times* (China), February. [link](https://www.globaltimes.cn/page/202402/1306668.shtml) — **Grade D3**. Summary: Argues US and UK strikes cannot end attacks because the cause is the Gaza war, presenting coalitions as ineffective; no figure. Reliability: Strongly anti-US party-state media; evidence of China's position, not of facts.
- **Global Times (2024). Impact of Red Sea tension on Persian Gulf shipping gradually diminishing as booking prices decline: Shanghai Shipping Exchange.** *Global Times* (China), January. [link](https://www.globaltimes.cn/page/202401/1305748.shtml) — **Grade D3**. Summary: Uses Shanghai Shipping Exchange data to argue knock-on freight effects were easing by January 2024. Reliability: Party-state media that tends to play down damage; check against Drewry or Freightos.
- **Xinhua (2026). Houthis say over 400 ships crossed Bab al-Mandab Strait in 10 days despite nearby fighting.** *Xinhua* (China), 24 September. [link](https://english.news.cn/20260924/d03e0a3c656941c99a64f39bd1404dd9/c.html) — **Grade D3**. Summary: Relays the Houthi claim that 408 ships passed between 11 and 21 September 2026 and that only Saudi vessels were banned. Reliability: Unverified Houthi figures passed on by Chinese state media; check against JMIC or Kpler.
- **Global Times (2024). US escalates Red Sea tensions, while China voices fairness.** *Global Times* (China), January. [link](https://www.globaltimes.cn/page/202401/1305514.shtml) — **Grade D4**. Summary: Contrasts US "escalation" with China's "fair" diplomacy and suggests ships showing Chinese identity were safe; no figure. Reliability: Propaganda framing contradicted by the March 2024 Houthi strike on a Chinese-owned tanker.


## Sources: Strait of Malacca

126 sources on Malacca and Singapore Strait piracy, ReCAAP, the joint patrols and the 2025–26 surge and fall. Most regional press is graded C: reliable for local detail and national views, but check figures against ReCAAP or IMB data.

### Peer-reviewed and academic

- **[Authors to check] (2023). Multilateral cooperation against maritime piracy in the Straits of Malacca: from the RMSI to ReCAAP.** *Marine Policy*. [link](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0308597X23001550) — **Grade A2**. Summary: Explains why the US-backed RMSI plan failed (Indonesia and Malaysia saw it as a threat to their sovereignty) and why ReCAAP, built on sharing information, did better. Reliability: Peer-reviewed journal with the abstract confirmed, but authors unknown, paywalled, and it may favour the ReCAAP model.
- **Johnson, D. and Valencia, M. (eds) (2005). Piracy in Southeast Asia: Status, Issues, and Responses.** ISEAS / IIAS (book). [link](https://bookshop.iseas.edu.sg/publication/744) — **Grade A2**. Summary: Academic book written at the height of the piracy peak, covering its causes, why sovereignty and border disputes slowed cooperation, and the role of shipowners and insurers. Reliability: Scholarly and written during the events, so it ends before ReCAAP and Eyes in the Sky took effect; summary from search results only.
- **Ong-Webb, G.G. (ed.) (2006). Piracy, Maritime Terrorism and Securing the Malacca Straits.** ISEAS / IIAS (book). [link](https://bookshop.iseas.edu.sg/publication/745) — **Grade A2**. Summary: 266-page academic book with a chapter on how ASEAN cooperation (MALSINDO, Eyes in the Sky) developed, published the year ReCAAP came into force. Reliability: Academic but from a Singapore-based publisher, so a Singapore viewpoint, and too early to judge long-term results.
- **Febrica, S. (2014). Securing the Sulu-Sulawesi Seas from maritime terrorism: a troublesome cooperation?** *Perspectives on Terrorism*. [link](https://strathprints.strath.ac.uk/76406/1/Febrica_POT_2014_Securing_the_Sulu_Sulawesi_sea_from_maratime.pdf) — **Grade A2**. Summary: Argues Indonesia rejected the US RMSI not only over sovereignty but because it seemed too military and Jakarta had no say in designing it. Reliability: Peer-reviewed article (repository copy), but the title is about the Sulu-Sulawesi Seas, so check that the Malacca/RMSI passage is really there.
- **[Authors to check] (2026). Complexities of governance and sovereignty in maritime security.** *Marine Policy*. [link](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/pii/S0308597X26000369) — **Grade A3**. Summary: Critiques Eyes in the Sky: about 8 air patrols a week were flown against about 70 needed, in daytime while pirates struck at night, so the fall in piracy does not prove the patrols worked. Reliability: Peer-reviewed journal, but the title and authors are unconfirmed, so check the 8/70 figures first.
- **[Authors to check] (2022). Interstate hostility and maritime crime: evidence from South East Asia.** *Marine Policy*. [link](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/am/pii/S0308597X22001816) — **Grade A3**. Summary: Statistical study linking bad relations between neighbours (no cross-border hot pursuit, distrust of foreign warships) to more armed robbery at sea: weak cooperation means more incidents. Reliability: Free manuscript of a peer-reviewed paper, but an exact-title search failed, so authors and details are unverified.
- **[Author to check] (2014). Multilateralism in the Straits of Malacca and Singapore.** *Marine Policy*. [link](https://www.sciencedirect.com/science/article/abs/pii/S0308597X13001978) — **Grade A6**.
